to the existence of a unique minimal
\vec{k}\in\Xi_{\min}^{+}

(with respect to the partial ordering induced by

\Xi_{\mathrm{Hasse}}
) such that
\rho
has a crystalline lift
of weight
(\vec{k},-\vec{1})
. The existence of such a
\vec{k}
is strongly suggested by its consistency with the Breuil–Mézard Conjecture in conjunction with modular representation theoretic considerations, and in particular Conjecture 2.3 of Wiersema’s thesis
[
Wie21a
]
(in the case that
p
is unramified in
F
).

•

Finally, the conjecture predicts that the minimal weights characterized in the preceding two points coincide; in particular if
\rho
arises from a mod
p
Hilbert modular eigenform of some weight, then the set of such weights is determined by the local Galois representations
\rho|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
for
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
.
More precisely, granting the “weak” conjecture and the existence of minimal weights as in parts (1) and (2), then their coincidence is equivalent to the following generalization of
[
DS23
, Conj. 7.3.2]
, which we may view as the “weight part” of the analogue of Serre’s conjecture in this context:

Conjecture 6.3.2
.

Suppose that
\rho:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
is irreducible and
geometrically modular some weight, and that
\vec{k}\in\Xi_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}
. Then

\rho
is geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
if and only if
\rho|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}

has a crystalline lift of weight
(\vec{k}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{m}_{\mathfrak{p}})
for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
.

Remark 6.3.3
.

We remark that the “only if” in Conjecture
6.3.2
may fail for certain
\vec{k}\not\in\Xi_{\min}^{+}
; see
[
Yan25b
, Example 3.2.21]
for an example due to Bartlett. On the other hand, the results in S. Yang’s thesis (see
[
Yan25b
, Rmk. 4.1.3]
) show that, for the purpose of ensuring the “only if” implication, the inequalities in the definition of the
\Xi_{\mathrm{min}}^{+}
are not sharp. (See also Remark
7.3.4
below.)

In any case, for a version that covers all
\vec{k}
, we could replace “weight
(\vec{k}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{m}_{\mathfrak{p}})
for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
” with “weight
(\vec{k}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{m}_{\mathfrak{p}})
for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
and some
\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\Xi_{\min}^{+}
such that
\vec{k}-\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\Xi_{\mathrm{Hasse}}
.”

6.4.
Dependence on
\vec{m}

We first note that the conjectural characterizations of

\vec{k}_{\min}(\rho,\vec{m})
depend only on a mod
p
character associated to
\vec{m}
.

Proposition 6.4.1
.

Let
\rho:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})

be an irreducible, continuous representation.
Suppose that
\vec{k},\vec{m},\vec{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
are such that
\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\omega_{\sigma}^{m_{\sigma}}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\omega_{\sigma}^{n_{\sigma}}
for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
,
where
\omega_{\sigma}:{\mathbb{F}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}\to\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times}
is the character induced by
\sigma
.

(1)

\rho
is geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
if and only if
\rho
is geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{n})
;

(2)

if
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
, then
\rho|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
has a crystalline lift of weight
(\vec{k}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{m}_{\mathfrak{p}})
if and only if it has a crystalline lift of weight
(\vec{k}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{n}_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

Proof.

First note that the condition on
\vec{m}
and
\vec{n}
means that
\vec{n}-\vec{m}\in\ker(\lambda)
, where

\lambda:\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}\longrightarrow\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}}{\operatorname{Hom}\,}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times})

is the surjective homomorphism defined by
\lambda(\vec{\ell})_{{\mathfrak{p}}}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\omega_{\sigma}^{\ell_{\sigma}}
.
Since
\vec{h}_{\sigma}\in\ker(\lambda)
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
,
and

[\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}:\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\mathbb{Z}\vec{h}_{\sigma}]=|\det(A)|=\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}}(p^{f_{\mathfrak{p}}}-1),

where
A
is the
d\times d
-matrix with columns
\vec{h}_{\sigma}
, it follows that
\ker(\lambda)=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\mathbb{Z}\vec{h}_{\sigma}
, and therefore
\vec{n}=\vec{m}+\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma}r_{\sigma}\vec{h}_{\sigma}
for some
\vec{r}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
.

Recall from §
4.1
that we have Hecke-equivariant isomorphisms

M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,E)\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}+\vec{h}_{\sigma}}(U,E)

for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
, so it follows that
M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,E)\cong M_{\vec{k},\vec{n}}(U,E)
, which implies (1).

To prove (2), recall that a character
\xi:G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}\to\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
(necessarily
\overline{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{p}^{\times}
-valued) is crystalline with
\sigma
-labelled Hodge–Tate weights
(w_{\sigma})
for
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
if and only if the restriction

{\mathcal{O}}_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}\to\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
of the character corresponding to
\xi
via local class field theory has the form

\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\sigma^{w_{\sigma}}
(see
[
Con11
, App. B]
).
The condition on
\vec{m}
and
\vec{n}
therefore implies that there is such a character
\xi
with trivial reduction and
\sigma
-labelled Hodge–Tate weights
(m_{\sigma}-n_{\sigma})
for
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Thus if
\widetilde{\rho}
is a crystalline lift of
\rho|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
of weight
(\vec{k}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{m}_{\mathfrak{p}})
, then
\xi\otimes\widetilde{\rho}
is
a crystalline lift of
\rho|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
of weight
(\vec{k}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{n}_{\mathfrak{p}})
.
∎

Let
Q
be a finite set of primes of
F
containing
S_{p}
, and
suppose that
\chi:G_{F}\to\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times}
is a continuous character unramified outside
Q
. Write
\xi:{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\times}/F^{\times}F_{\infty,+}^{\times}\to\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times}

for the character corresponding to
\chi
via class field theory, so in particular,
\xi(\varpi_{v})=\chi({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v})
for all
v\not\in Q
. Note also that
\chi
is tamely ramified at all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
, and that
\xi|_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}}
induces the character
{\mathbb{F}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}\to\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times}
corresponding to
\chi|_{I_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
via local class field theory.

Proposition 6.4.2
.

Let
\rho:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
be an irreducible, continuous representation, and let
\chi:G_{F}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\times}
be a continuous character. Suppose that
\vec{k},\vec{\ell},\vec{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
, and that
\xi|_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\overline{\sigma}^{-\ell_{\sigma}}
for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
, where
\xi
is the character corresponding to
\chi
via class field theory.

(1)

\rho
is geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
if and only if
\chi\otimes\rho
is geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{\ell}+\vec{m})
;

(2)

if
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
, then
\rho|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
has a crystalline lift of weight
(\vec{k}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{m}_{\mathfrak{p}})
if and only if
(\chi\otimes\rho)|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
has a crystalline lift of weight
(\vec{k}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{\ell}_{\mathfrak{p}}+\vec{m}_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

In particular, Conjectures
6.3.1
and
6.3.2
hold for
\rho
if and only if they hold for
\chi\otimes\rho
.

Proof.

(1) Suppose that
\rho
is geometrically of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
, and let
f\in M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,E)
be an eigenform such that
\rho_{f}
is isomorphic to
\rho
. Note that since
\xi
is trivial on
F^{\times}F_{\infty,+}^{\times}
, its restriction to
(\mathbb{A}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})^{\times}
satisfies
\xi(\alpha)=\alpha^{\vec{\ell}}
for all
\alpha\in{\mathcal{O}}_{F,(p),+}^{\times}
. Therefore, shrinking
U
and enlarging
E
as necessary, we have the isomorphism

e_{\xi}:M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,E)\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}M_{\vec{k},\vec{\ell}+\vec{m}}(U,E)

of (
3
), and hence an eigenform
\varphi=e_{\xi}f\in M_{\vec{k},\vec{\ell}+\vec{m}}(U,E)
such that

T_{v}(\varphi)=\xi(\varpi_{v})a_{v}\varphi
and
S_{v}(\varphi)=\xi(\varpi_{v})^{2}d_{v}\varphi
for all but finitely many primes
v
of
F
, where
a_{v}={\operatorname{tr}\,}(\rho({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v}))
and
\mathrm{Nm}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(v)d_{v}=\det(\rho({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v}))
. It follows that
\rho_{\varphi}
is isomorphic to
\chi\otimes\rho_{f}
, and therefore that
\chi\otimes\rho
is modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{\ell}+\vec{m})
.

The converse holds by symmetry.

(2) We claim that
\chi|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
has a crystalline lift
\widetilde{\chi}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
whose
\sigma
-labelled Hodge–Tate weight is
-\ell_{\sigma}
. Indeed, this follows from
[
Con11
, App. B]

on taking
\widetilde{\chi}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
to correspond via local class field theory to a lift of
\xi|_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}
whose restriction to
{\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}
is
\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\sigma^{-\ell_{\sigma}}
. Therefore if
\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a crystalline lift of
\rho|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
of weight
(\vec{k}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{m}_{\mathfrak{p}})
, then

\widetilde{\chi}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a crystalline lift of
(\chi\otimes\rho)|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
of weight
(\vec{k}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{\ell}_{\mathfrak{p}}+\vec{m}_{\mathfrak{p}})
. This gives one direction of the implication, and the converse again holds by symmetry.
∎

Remark 6.4.3
.

Recall that conjectures along the lines of those above are formulated in
[
DS23
]
under the assumption that
p
is unramified in
F
. However, due to the different conventions in the definition of the Hecke operators, the Galois representation associated to an eigenform
f
in
[
DS23
]
is
\chi\otimes\rho_{f}
in the notation of this paper, where
\chi
is the cyclotomic character (see Remark
5.2.2
). Therefore
\rho
is modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
in the notation of
[
DS23
]
if and only if
\chi^{-1}\otimes\rho
is modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
in the notation of this paper, which, by the preceding proposition, is equivalent to
\rho
being modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m}-\vec{1})

The analogous statement holds for our conventions with respect to weights of crystalline lifts, i.e.,
\rho|_{G_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
has a crystalline lift of weight
(\vec{k}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{m}_{\mathfrak{p}})
in the notation of
[
DS23
]
if and only if it has a crystalline lift of weight
(\vec{k}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{m}_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)
in the notation of this paper.

It follows that if
p
is unramified in
F
, then Conjectures
6.3.1
and
6.3.2
above are equivalent to Conjectures 7.3.1 and 7.3.2 of
[
DS23
]
, but with
\vec{k}_{\min}(\rho,\vec{m})
of that paper equal to

\vec{k}_{\min}(\chi^{-1}\otimes\rho,\vec{m})=\vec{k}_{\min}(\rho,\vec{m}-\vec{1})
of this paper (where
\chi
is the cyclotomic character).

Finally we note the following consequence of the effect of

\Theta
-operators.

Proposition 6.4.4
.

Let
\rho:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
be an irreducible, continuous representation. Suppose that

\vec{k},\vec{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
and
\varsigma\in\Sigma
. If
\rho
is geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
,
then
\rho
is geometrically modular of weight

(\vec{k}+\vec{h}_{\varsigma}+2\vec{e}_{\varsigma},\vec{m}-\vec{e}_{\varsigma})
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\rho
is geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
, and let
f\in M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,E)
be an eigenform giving rise to
\rho
. We first treat that the case where
\varsigma=\sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
for some
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{0}
. We wish to reduce to the case that
f\not\in\ker(\Theta_{\tau})
. Recall that
\ker(\Theta_{\tau})
is described in Theorem
4.2.2
in terms of the images of the maps

V_{\mathfrak{p}}:M_{\vec{\ell},\vec{n}}(U,E)\hookrightarrow M_{\vec{\ell}^{\prime},\vec{n}^{\prime}}(U,E)
,
where
\vec{\ell}^{\prime}=\vec{\ell}+\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\ell_{\sigma}\vec{h}_{\sigma}
and
\vec{n}^{\prime}=\vec{n}+\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}n_{\sigma}\vec{h}_{\sigma}
.

First we claim that we may assume
f
is not divisible by any partial Hasse invariant
H_{\sigma}
. Indeed, we may write
f=\phi\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}H_{\sigma}^{r_{\sigma}}
for some
\vec{r}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}_{\geq 0}
and
\phi\in M_{\vec{k}^{\prime},\vec{m}}(U,E)
not divisible by any
H_{\sigma}
, where
\vec{k}^{\prime}=\vec{k}_{\min}(f)
and
\vec{k}-\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\Xi_{\mathrm{Hasse}}
. If the proposition holds for
\phi
, then we have that
\rho
is geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k}^{\prime}+\vec{h}_{\varsigma}+2\vec{e}_{\varsigma},\vec{m}-\vec{e}_{\varsigma})
, and hence of weight
(\vec{k}+\vec{h}_{\varsigma}+2\vec{e}_{\varsigma},\vec{m}-\vec{e}_{\varsigma})
by Proposition
6.1.2
.

Next we show that there is a maximal non-negative integer
n
such that

f=V_{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}(\phi)\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}G_{\sigma}^{r_{\sigma}}

for some
\phi\in M_{\vec{k}^{\prime},\vec{m}^{\prime}}(U,E)
,
\vec{k}^{\prime},\vec{m}^{\prime},\vec{r}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
.
Indeed since
V_{\mathfrak{p}}
and multiplication by the
G_{\sigma}
are Hecke-equivariant, the formula implies that
\phi
is an eigenform giving rise to
\rho
, whose irreducibility then implies that
\vec{k}^{\prime}\neq\vec{0}
. Furthermore since
f
is not divisible by any
H_{\sigma}
, neither is
\phi
(see
[
Dia23
, §9.3]
for the effect of
V_{\mathfrak{p}}
on the
H_{\sigma}
and
G_{\sigma}
), so Theorem
4.2.3
implies that
\vec{k}^{\prime}=\vec{k}_{\min}(\phi)\in\Xi_{\min}^{+}
. The boundedness of
n
then follows from the fact that in the definition of
V_{\mathfrak{p}}
, if each
\ell_{\sigma}>0
, then
\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\ell^{\prime}_{\sigma}>\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\ell_{\sigma}
.

Now note that for
\phi
as above, we have
\Theta_{\tau}(\phi)\neq 0
. Indeed, if
\phi\in\ker(\Theta_{\tau})
, then Theorem
4.2.2
implies that

\phi=V_{\mathfrak{p}}(g)\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}G_{\sigma}^{s_{\sigma}}

for some
g\in M_{\vec{\ell},\vec{n}}(U,E)
,
\vec{\ell},\vec{n},\vec{s}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
(since
\phi
is not divisible by any
H_{\sigma}
), which contradicts the characterization of
n
.
Therefore
\rho
arises from
\Theta_{\tau}(\phi)
, and hence is geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k}^{\prime}+\vec{h}_{\varsigma}+2\vec{e}_{\varsigma},\vec{m}^{\prime}-\vec{e}_{\varsigma})
.
Since
\vec{k}-\vec{k}^{\prime}\in\Xi_{\mathrm{Hasse}}
and
\lambda(\vec{m})=\lambda(\vec{m}^{\prime})
(in the notation of the proof of Proposition
6.4.1
), it follows
that
\rho
is geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k}+\vec{h}_{\varsigma}+2\vec{e}_{\varsigma},\vec{m}-\vec{e}_{\varsigma})

(by Propositions
6.1.2
and
6.4.1
).

Finally suppose that
\varsigma=\sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
for some
j<e_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Note that

(\vec{h}_{\varsigma}+2\vec{e}_{\varsigma})-(\vec{h}_{\varsigma^{\prime}}+2\vec{e}_{\varsigma^{\prime}})=\vec{h}_{\varsigma}+\vec{h}_{\varsigma^{\prime}}\in\Xi_{\mathrm{Hasse}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\lambda(\vec{e}_{\varsigma})=\lambda(\vec{e}_{\varsigma^{\prime}}),

where
\varsigma=\sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j+1}
.
The desired conclusion therefore follows (by induction on
e_{\mathfrak{p}}-j
) from Propositions
6.1.2
and
6.4.1
.
∎

Remark 6.4.5
.

Under the assumption that
k_{\sigma}\geq 2
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
, a statement analogous to Proposition
6.4.4
for crystalline lifts follows from the Breuil–Mézard Conjecture and a modular representation theory calculation. In the same vein, so does an analogue of Proposition
6.1.2
, under the further assumption that
\vec{k}
and
\vec{k}+\vec{\ell}\in\Xi_{\min}
.

Remark 6.4.6
.

If there exist
\vec{k}_{\min}(\rho,\vec{m})
as in part (1) of Conjecture
6.3.1
, then Proposition
6.4.4
implies that

\vec{k}_{\min}(\rho,\vec{m}-\vec{e}_{\sigma})\leq\vec{k}_{\min}(\rho,\vec{m})+\vec{h}_{\sigma}+2\vec{e}_{\sigma}

where
\sigma=\sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}
and the partial ordering is defined by
\vec{k}\leq\vec{k}^{\prime}
if
\vec{k}^{\prime}-\vec{k}\in\Xi_{\mathrm{Hasse}}
. (The same holds for

\sigma=\sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}
if
j<e_{\mathfrak{p}}
, but in view of the final step in the proof of Proposition
6.4.4
, this is a weaker assertion.) Furthermore, it follows from
[
Dia23
, Thm. 5.2.1]
and the proof of Proposition
6.4.4
that if
\vec{k}=\vec{k}_{\min}(\rho,\vec{m})
satisfies
p|k_{\sigma}
, then in fact

\vec{k}_{\min}(\rho,\vec{m}-\vec{e}_{\sigma})\leq\vec{k}(\rho,\vec{m})+2\vec{e}_{\sigma}.

Recall also from Proposition
6.4.1
(and its proof) that
\vec{k}_{\min}(\rho,\vec{m})
will depend only on the image
\lambda(\vec{m})\in{\operatorname{Hom}\,}(T,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times})
, where
T=\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}}{\mathbb{F}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}
. We may view the resulting function

{\operatorname{Hom}\,}(T,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times})\to\Xi_{\min}^{+}

(defined by
\lambda(\vec{m})\to\vec{k}_{\min}(\rho,\vec{m})
)
as a generalized “
\Theta
-cycle.” Note however that the information is slightly cruder than what is provided by the original notion in the classical setting (as in
[
Edi92
, §3]
), since it fails to distinguish between ordinary and non-ordinary forms giving rise to the same Galois representation.

6.5.
Algebraic modularity

We now discuss the relation with “algebraic” Serre weight conjectures in this context, as formulated in
[
BDJ10
]
for
p
unramified in
F
, and then generalized to the ramified case by Schein
[
Sch08
]
and Gee
[
Gee11a
]
. Recall that (for
F
unramified at
p
) a weight
V
in
[
BDJ10
]
is an
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}
-representation of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/p)
, and the notion of a representation
\rho:G_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
being modular of weight
V
is defined terms of the manifestation of
\rho
in Jacobians of certain Shimura curves over
F
, or equivalently, the appearance of
\rho
in their cohomology with coefficients in an étale sheaf determined by
V
(see
[
BDJ10
, Lemma 2.4]
).
Furthermore, this is in turn equivalent to the presence of the corresponding system of Hecke eigenvalues arising in such cohomology (see
[
BDJ10
, Lemmas 4.10, 4.11]
). One can make similar definitions in terms of Hecke eigensystems on forms associated to totally definite quaternion algebras (as in
[
Gee11b
]
).

To adapt these notions to our setting, let
{\mathcal{O}}_{B}
be a maximal order in a quaternion algebra
B
over
F
, where
B
is as in §
3.6
or §
3.7
, so
B
is unramified at all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
and at most one archimedean place of
F
. In either case, we defined a space
M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}^{B}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
for any
\vec{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}^{\Sigma}
,
\vec{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
and sufficiently small open compact
U\subset B_{{\mathbb{A}},\mathbf{f}}^{\times}
containing
{\mathcal{O}}_{B,p}^{\times}
, and equipped it with an action of (commuting) Hecke operators
T_{v}
and
S_{v}
for all primes
v\not\in S_{p}
such that
B
is unramified at
v
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{B,v}^{\times}\subset U
. We thus have an action of
\mathbb{T}^{Q}
on
M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}^{B}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
, where
\mathbb{T}^{Q}
is the polynomial ring over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}
in the variables
T_{v}
and
S_{v}
for all
v\not\in Q
, and
Q
is any finite set of primes of
F
containing all
v
such that
v\in S_{p}
,
B_{v}^{\times}
is ramified, or
{\mathcal{O}}_{B,v}^{\times}\not\subset U
.

Suppose now that
\rho:G_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
is any continuous, irreducible, totally odd representation, and
Q
is a finite set of primes containing all those in
S_{p}
and those at which
\rho
is ramified. We let
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q}
denote the maximal ideal of
\mathbb{T}^{Q}

generated by the elements

T_{v}-{\operatorname{tr}\,}(\rho({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v}))\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathrm{Nm}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(v)S_{v}-\det(\rho({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v}))

for all
v\not\in Q
.

We then make the following definition:

Definition 6.5.1
.

For
\rho
,
\vec{k}
and
\vec{m}
as above, we say that
\rho
is
definitely modular
(resp.
algebraically modular
) of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
if

M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}^{B}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})[{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q}]\neq 0

for some
B
,
U
and
Q
as above, where
B
is totally definite (resp. unramified at exactly one archimedean place).

Note that since
M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}^{B}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
is finite-dimensional, the action on it of
\mathbb{T}^{Q}
factors through an Artinian quotient. It follows that the
condition in the definition is equivalent to
the non-vanishing of
M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}^{B}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q}}
, or equivalently the containment
{\operatorname{Ann}\,}_{\mathbb{T}^{Q}}(M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}^{B}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}))\subset{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q}
.

We note also that if
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q}
is in the support of
M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}^{B}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
, then so is
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q^{\prime}}
for all finite
Q^{\prime}\supset Q
. Furthermore, if
{\mathcal{O}}_{B,p}^{\times}\subset U^{\prime}\subset U
and
Q^{\prime}
contains all
v
such that
{\mathcal{O}}_{B,v}^{\times}\not\subset U^{\prime}
, then
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q^{\prime}}
is also in the support of
M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}^{B}(U^{\prime},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
. This is clear if
B
is totally definite; otherwise, we need to show that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q^{\prime}}
is not in the kernel of
the map
M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}^{B}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})\to M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}^{B}(U^{\prime},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
induced by the projection
Y_{U^{\prime}}^{B}\to Y_{U}^{B}
, for which we may assume that
U^{\prime}
is normal in
U
. As in the proof of Theorem
5.2.1
, the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence identifies this kernel with

H^{1}(G,H^{0}(Y_{U^{\prime}}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}))
as a
\mathbb{T}^{Q^{\prime}}
-module, where
G=U/U^{\prime}(U\cap{\mathcal{O}}_{F}^{\times})
, and a standard argument shows that
H^{0}(Y_{U^{\prime}}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q^{\prime}}}=0
(see for example the proof of Lemma
6.5.3
below).

The above notion of algebraic modularity generalizes the one in
[
BDJ10
]
and
[
DS23
]
, but with different conventions.
More precisely, if
p
is unramified in
F
, then
\rho
is algebraically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
in the sense above if and only if it is algebraically modular of
(\vec{k},\vec{m}+\vec{1})
in the sense of
[
DS23
]
, or equivalently modular of weight
D_{\vec{k},-\vec{k}-\vec{m}-\vec{1},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}

in the sense of
[
BDJ10
]
, where

D_{\vec{k},-\vec{k}-\vec{m}-\vec{1},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}=\bigotimes_{\sigma\in\Sigma}(\det\nolimits^{-k_{\sigma}-m_{\sigma}}\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}\operatorname{Sym}^{k_{\sigma}-2}\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{2})

(as defined in §
3.6
) is viewed as a representation of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/p{\mathcal{O}}_{F})
.

We note that part (1) of both Propositions
6.4.1
and
6.4.2
hold with “geometrically” replaced by either “definitely” or “algebraically.” Indeed in the case of Proposition
6.4.1
, this is immediate from the definitions, and for Proposition
6.4.2
, the same proof as for geometric modularity carries over.

Recall that Conjecture 7.5.2 of
[
DS23
]

predicts (for
p
unramified in
F
) that if
\rho
is algebraically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
(where
\vec{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}_{\geq 2})
, then it is algebraically modular of the same weight, and that the converse holds if in addition
\vec{k}\in\Xi_{\rm min}
.
We extend the conjecture to the current setting as follows:

Conjecture 6.5.2
.

Suppose that
\rho:G_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
is a continuous, irreducible, totally odd representation, and that
(\vec{k},\vec{m})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}_{\geq 2}\times\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
.

(1)

\rho
is definitely modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
if and only if
\rho
is algebraically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
.

(2)

If
\rho
is algebraically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
then
\rho
is geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
; furthermore the converse holds if
\vec{k}\in\Xi_{\rm min}
.

Under a Taylor–Wiles hypothesis on
\rho
, part (1) follows from results of Gee–Liu–Savitt
[
GLS15
]
(see Theorem
6.5.9
and Corollary
6.5.10
below). As explained in
[
DS23
]
, the first assertion in (2) is straightforward if all
k_{\sigma}
have the same parity. Here we prove the forward directions of (1) and (2) unconditionally. The other direction of (2) is more difficult, but some cases are proved in S. Yang’s PhD thesis
[
Yan25b
]
(see also
[
Yan25a
]
).

Before proving the implications, we recall the following well-known behavior of weights with respect to definite (resp. algebraic) modularity. Note that the action of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F,p})
on
D_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
factors through
\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}}\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
, and recall that this representation is irreducible if and only if
\vec{k}
has the property that for each
\tau=\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}\in\Sigma_{0}
, we have

\mbox{$2\leq k_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j_{\tau}}\leq p+1$ for some $j_{\tau}\in{1,\ldots,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}$,}\quad\mbox{and $k_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}=2$ for all $j\neq j_{\tau}$}.

Observe also that such a
D_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
depends only on the pair
(\lambda(\vec{m}),\vec{k}^{0})
, where

\lambda(\vec{m})
is a character of
\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}}{\mathbb{F}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}
(as in the proof of Proposition
6.4.1
) and
\vec{k}^{0}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma_{0}}
is defined by
k^{0}_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}=\max\{k_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,1},\ldots,k_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}\}
. In particular, it does not depend on the
j_{\tau}
such that
k^{0}_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}=k_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j_{\tau}}
(but such a
\vec{k}
is in
\Xi_{\rm min}
if and only if
k^{0}_{{\mathfrak{p}},i}=k_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,e_{\mathfrak{p}}}
for all
\tau\in\Sigma_{0}
). Recall also that for such
\vec{k}
, the representation
D_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
is dual to
D_{\vec{k},-\vec{k}-\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
. The following lemma reduces the question of definite (resp. algebraic) modularity to consideration of such weights.

Lemma 6.5.3
.

Suppose that
\rho:G_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
is a continuous, irreducible, totally odd representation, and that
(\vec{k},\vec{m})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}_{\geq 2}\times\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
. Then
\rho
is definitely (resp. algebraically) modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
if and only if
\rho
is definitely (resp. algebraically) modular of some weight
(\vec{\ell},\vec{n})
such that
D_{\vec{\ell},\vec{n},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
is a Jordan–Hölder factor of
D_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
.

Proof.

We only sketch the argument, which is standard.

Consider first the case of algebraic modularity. Adopting the notation of §
3.7
, an exact sequence
0\to T\to T^{\prime}\to T^{\prime\prime}\to 0
of
{\mathcal{O}}
-modules with smooth actions of
U_{p}={\mathcal{O}}_{B,p}^{\times}
yields an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow 0

of locally constant sheaves on
Y_{B}^{U}
, where
U=U_{p}U^{p}
for sufficiently small
U^{p}
. Furthermore the morphisms in the resulting long exact sequence

H^{0}(Y_{U}^{B},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime})\to H^{1}(Y_{U}^{B},{\mathcal{T}})\to H^{1}(Y_{U}^{B},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\to H^{1}(Y_{U}^{B},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime})\to H^{2}(Y_{U}^{B},{\mathcal{T}})

are
\mathbb{T}^{Q}
-equivariant. It follows that if
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q}
is in the support of
M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})=H^{1}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})
, then it is in the support of

M_{\vec{\ell},\vec{n}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})=H^{1}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{\ell},\vec{n},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})
for some Jordan–Hölder factor
D_{\vec{\ell},\vec{n},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
of
D_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
.

To prove the converse, it suffices to show that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q}
is not in the support of
H^{i}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{\ell},\vec{n},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})
for any such Jordan-Hölder factor and
i=0
or
2
. Furthermore, we claim that these spaces vanish for such
\ell
, unless
\vec{\ell}=\vec{0}
. For
i=0
, this follows from the fact that the sections on each connected component are identified with the invariants in
D_{\vec{\ell},\vec{n},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
under the action of
B_{+}^{\times}\cap gUg^{-1}
for some
g\in(B_{\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})^{\times}
, and the image of this group in

\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}}\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
contains
\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
by the Strong Approximation Theorem. The vanishing for
i=2
then follows from Poincaré Duality (and the duality between
D_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
and
D_{\vec{k},-\vec{k}-\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
).

We now assume
\vec{\ell}=\vec{0}
and analyze the Hecke action on
H^{0}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{0},\vec{n},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})
by identifying it with the space of
U^{p}
-invariants in the smooth
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}
-representation

\varinjlim H^{0}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{0},\vec{n},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})=\varinjlim H^{0}(C_{U}^{B},\pi_{U,*}\mathcal{D}_{\vec{0},\vec{n},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})

of
(B_{\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})^{\times}
, where the limit is over sufficiently small
U^{p}
and
\pi_{U}:Y_{U}^{B}\to C_{U}^{B}=\mathbb{A}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{\times}/F_{+}^{\times}\det(U)
is the projection to the set of connected components. Furthermore
\pi_{U,*}\mathcal{D}_{\vec{0},\vec{n},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
may be identified with the locally constant sheaf over
C_{U}
defined by sections of

(\mathbb{A}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{\times}\times\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}(\xi_{\vec{n}}))/F_{+}^{\times}\det(U),

where the character
\xi_{\vec{n}}
of
F_{+}^{\times}\det(U)
is trivial on
F_{+}^{\times}\det(U^{p})
and (the inflation of)
x\mapsto x^{\vec{n}}
on
{\mathcal{O}}_{F,p}^{\times}
. In particular, it follows that if
v\not\in Q
splits completely in the abelian extension
L
of
F
corresponding via class field theory to
\ker(\xi_{\vec{n}})
, then

T_{v}=1+\mathrm{Nm}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(v)\quad\mbox{and}\quad S_{v}=1

on
H^{0}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{0},\vec{n},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})
.
So if
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q}
lies in its support, then the Cebotarev Density and Brauer–Nesbitt Theorems imply that
\rho|_{G_{L}}^{\mathrm{ss}}
is isomorphic to
1+\chi_{{\operatorname{cyc}\,}}^{-1}
, which in turn implies that
\rho
is reducible, yielding the desired contradiction.
Using Poincaré Duality, we similarly obtain a contradiction to
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q}
being in the support of
H^{2}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{0},\vec{n},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})
.

The proof for definite modularity is similar, but much easier, since it only involves
H^{0}
. The result therefore follows directly from the exactness of sequences of the form

0\longrightarrow H^{0}(Y_{U}^{B},{\mathcal{T}})\longrightarrow H^{0}(Y_{U}^{B},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\longrightarrow H^{0}(Y_{U}^{B},{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime})\longrightarrow 0.

∎

We recall also an analogue of Ihara’s Lemma in the setting of totally definite quaternion algebras, essentially due to Taylor
[
Tay89
]
. Let
B
be a totally definite quaternion algebra over
F
unramified at all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
,
U
a sufficiently small open compact subgroup of
B_{{\mathbb{A}},\mathbf{f}}^{\times}
containing
{\mathcal{O}}_{B,p}^{\times}
, and
Q
a sufficiently large finite set of primes of
F
. For
w\not\in Q
, choose an element
h\in{\mathcal{O}}_{B,w}\cong M_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F,w})
such that
\det(h)
is a uniformizer, let
U_{0}(w)=U\cap hUh^{-1}
, and consider the level-raising map

\beta:M^{B}_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})^{2}\longrightarrow M^{B}_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U_{0}(w),\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})

defined by
\beta(f_{1},f_{2})(x)=f_{1}(x)+f_{2}(xh)
. Note that
\beta
is
\mathbb{T}^{Q^{\prime}}
-linear, where
Q^{\prime}=Q\cup\{w\}
.

The analogue of Ihara’s Lemma is the following, for which our proof is based on the one in
[
DT94
, Lemma 2]
.

Lemma 6.5.4
.

Suppose that
B
,
U
,
Q
,
w
and
\beta
are as above, and
\rho
,
\vec{k}
and
\vec{m}
are as in Conjecture
6.5.2
. Then
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q^{\prime}}
is not in the support of
\ker(\beta)
.

Proof.

If
f_{1}
,
f_{2}\in M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}^{B}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
are such that
{\beta}(f_{1},f_{2})=0
, then
f_{1}(x)=-f_{2}(xh)
for all
x\in B_{{\mathbb{A}}}^{\times}
. It follows that
f_{1}(\gamma_{\mathbf{f}}xu)=u_{p}^{-1}f_{1}(x)
for all
\gamma\in B^{\times}
,
x\in B_{\mathbf{f}}^{\times}
and
u\in\langle U,h^{-1}Uh\rangle=UB_{w}^{1}
,
where
B_{w}^{1}
is the kernel of
\det:B_{w}^{\times}\to F_{w}^{\times}
. In particular,
f_{1}(\gamma_{\mathbf{f}}xu)=f_{1}(x)
for all
\gamma\in B^{\times}
,
x\in B_{\mathbf{f}}^{\times}
and
u\in U^{\prime}B_{w}^{1}
,
where
U^{\prime}=U^{p}U^{\prime}_{p}
and
U_{p}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{B_{p}}^{\times}
is the principal congruence subgroup of level
\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}}{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Suppose now that
v\not\in Q^{\prime}
splits completely in the abelian extension
L
of
F
corresponding via class field theory to
F_{+}^{\times}\det(U^{\prime})
. Then for any
x\in B_{\mathbf{f}}^{\times}
and
g\in B_{v}^{\times}
, the Strong Approximation Theorem implies that
xg=\gamma_{\mathbf{f}}xu
for some
\gamma\in B^{\times}
,
u\in U^{\prime}B_{w}^{1}
, from which it follows that
T_{v}f_{1}=(\mathrm{Nm}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(v)+1)f_{1}=2f_{1}
and
S_{v}f_{1}=f_{1}
. Thus if
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q^{\prime}}
is in the support of
\ker(\beta)
, then
T_{v}-2,S_{v}-1\in{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q^{\prime}}
for all such
v
, which (by the Chebotarev Density and Brauer–Nesbitt Theorems) implies that
\rho|_{G_{L}}
has trivial semi-simplification, contradicting the irreducibility of
\rho
.
∎

We now prove one direction of each part of Conjecture
6.5.2
.

Theorem 6.5.5
.

Suppose that
\rho
,
\vec{k}
and
\vec{m}
are as in Conjecture
6.5.2
.

(1)

If
\rho
is definitely modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
,
then
\rho
is algebraically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
.

(2)

If
\rho
is algebraically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
, then
\rho
is geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
.

Proof.

(1) Firstly, by Lemma
6.5.3
, we may assume that
D_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
is irreducible, and in particular that
2\leq k_{\sigma}\leq p+1
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
.

Let
B
,
U
and
Q
be as in Definition
6.5.1
, with
B
totally definite, so there exists a non-zero
f\in M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
(for some sufficiently small open compact subgroup
U
of level prime to
p
and sufficiently large finite set of primes
Q
) such that
T_{v}f=a_{v}f
and
S_{v}f=d_{v}f
for all
v\not\in Q
, where

a_{v}={\operatorname{tr}\,}(\rho({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v}))\quad\mbox{and}\quad d_{v}=\mathrm{Nm}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(v)^{-1}\det(\rho({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v})).

As usual, we choose a character
\xi:({\mathbb{A}}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})^{\times}\to\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times}
such that
\xi(\alpha)=\alpha^{\vec{m}+\vec{k}/2}
for all
\alpha\in{\mathcal{O}}_{F,(p),+}^{\times}
and (after shrinking
U
and enlarging
Q
as needed) apply the isomorphism

e_{\xi}:M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}M^{B}_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})

to obtain an eigenform
\varphi\in M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
such that
T_{v}\varphi=a_{v}^{\prime}\varphi
and
S_{v}\varphi=d_{v}^{\prime}\varphi
for all
v\not\in Q
, where
a_{v}^{\prime}=\xi(\varpi_{v})^{-1}a_{v}
and
d_{v}^{\prime}=\xi(\varpi_{v})^{-2}d_{v}
.

Let
L=\overline{F}^{\ker(\rho)}
denote the splitting field of
\rho
. By the Chebotarev Density Theorem, we may choose a prime
w\not\in Q
such that the conjugacy class of
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{w}
in
{\operatorname{Gal}\,}(L(\zeta_{p})/F)
is that of a complex conjugation. Since
\rho
is totally odd and
\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{w})=-1
, we have
a_{w}={\operatorname{tr}\,}(\rho({\operatorname{Frob}}_{w}))=0
and
\mathrm{Nm}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(w)\equiv-1\bmod p
.

Now consider the level-raising map

\beta^{\prime}:M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,{\mathcal{O}})^{2}\longrightarrow M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U_{0}(w),{\mathcal{O}})

defined by
\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})(x)=\varphi_{1}(x)+\varphi_{2}(xh)
, where
U_{0}(w)
and
h
are as in the definition of
\beta
(preceding Lemma
6.5.4
). Thus
\beta^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{T}
-linear, where
\mathbb{T}
is the polynomial ring over
{\mathcal{O}}
in the variables
T_{v}
and
S_{v}
for
v\not\in Q^{\prime}=Q\cup\{w\}
. Let
{\mathfrak{m}}
denote the kernel of the homomorphism
\mathbb{T}\to E
defined by
T_{v}\mapsto a_{v}^{\prime}
,
S_{v}\mapsto d_{v}^{\prime}
. It follows from Lemma
6.5.4
and the commutativity of the diagram

\textstyle{M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})_{{\mathfrak{m}}}^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{(e_{\xi},\,\xi(\varpi_{w})^{-1}e_{\xi})}
\textstyle{M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U_{0}(w),\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})_{{\mathfrak{m}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{e_{\xi}}
\textstyle{M^{B}_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q^{\prime}}}^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q^{\prime}}}}
\textstyle{M^{B}_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U_{0}(w),\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q^{\prime}}}}

that the reduction

\overline{\beta}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{m}}}:M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,E)^{2}_{{\mathfrak{m}}}\longrightarrow M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U_{0}(w),E)_{{\mathfrak{m}}}

of
\beta^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{m}}}
is injective, and hence that
\beta^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{m}}}
is injective with torsion-free cokernel.

Let us note also that the same argument, applied with
\vec{m}
replaced by
-\vec{k}-\vec{m}
,
\rho
replaced by
\chi_{\operatorname{cyc}\,}^{-1}\otimes\rho^{\vee}
and
\xi
replaced by
\xi^{-1}
shows that the same conclusions hold with
{\mathfrak{m}}
replaced by
{\mathfrak{n}}
, where
{\mathfrak{n}}
is the kernel of the homomorphism
\mathbb{T}\to E
defined by
T_{v}\mapsto d_{v}^{\prime-1}a_{v}^{\prime}
,
S_{v}\mapsto d_{v}^{\prime-1}
.

Next recall that since
2\leq k_{\sigma}\leq p+1
for all
\sigma
, the
{\mathcal{O}}_{B,(p)}^{\times}
-module
D_{\vec{k},E}
is equipped with a perfect
E
-bilinear pairing

\langle\,\,\,,\,\,\rangle:D_{\vec{k},E}\times D_{\vec{k},E}\to E

satisfying
\langle\gamma a,\gamma b\rangle=\langle a,b\rangle
for all
\gamma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{B,(p)}^{\times}
,
a,b\in D_{\vec{k},E}
(see
[
Jar13
]
for an explicit description).
We thus have a perfect pairing on
M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U^{\prime},E)
, for any sufficiently small
U^{\prime}
containing
{\mathcal{O}}_{B,p}^{\times}
, defined by

\langle\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\rangle=\sum_{x}\langle\varphi_{1}(x),\varphi_{2}(x)\rangle

where the sum is over double coset representatives, i.e.,

(B_{\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})^{\times}=\coprod_{x}{\mathcal{O}}_{B,(p)}^{\times}xU^{\prime p}
.
One sees also that for any
g\in(B_{\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})^{\times}
, the adjoint of the double coset operator
[UgU^{\prime}]
is
[U^{\prime}g^{-1}U]
.
In particular, the adjoint of
T_{v}
(resp.
S_{v}
) on
M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,E)
is
S_{v}^{-1}T_{v}
(resp.
S_{v}^{-1}
) for all
v\not\in Q
.
Furthermore, extending the pairing diagonally to a
perfect pairing on
M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,E)^{2}
, the adjoint of

\overline{\beta}^{\prime}
is the map

\overline{\alpha}^{\prime}=([U_{0}(w)1U],[U_{0}(w)h^{-1}U]):M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U_{0}(w),E)\longrightarrow M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,E)^{2}

In particular
\overline{\alpha}^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{T}
-linear, and
\overline{\alpha}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{m}}}
is surjective (since
\overline{\beta}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{n}}}
is injective).

Now consider the composite

\overline{\alpha}^{\prime}\circ\overline{\beta}^{\prime}
, which is the
\mathbb{T}
-linear endomorphism of
M_{\vec{k}}(U,E)^{2}
given by the matrix

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathrm{Nm}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(w)+1&T_{w}\\
S_{w}^{-1}T_{w}&\mathrm{Nm}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(w)+1\end{array}\right).

Since
(\varphi,0)\in\ker(\overline{\alpha}^{\prime}\circ\overline{\beta}^{\prime})[{\mathfrak{m}}]
, the localization
\overline{\alpha}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{m}}}\circ\overline{\beta}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{m}}}
is not an isomorphism, so its cokernel is non-trivial. Since
\overline{\alpha}^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{m}}}
is surjective, it follows that
\beta^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{m}}}
has non-trivial cokernel. Furthermore, since this cokernel is torsion-free, it follows that there is a prime ideal
{\mathfrak{p}}
of
\mathbb{T}
in the support of
{\operatorname{coker}\,}(\beta^{\prime})
such that
{\mathfrak{p}}\cap{\mathcal{O}}=0
, and hence (enlarging
K
if necessary), an eigenform

\widetilde{\varphi}\in{\operatorname{coker}\,}(\beta^{\prime})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}K
such that that
T_{v}\widetilde{\varphi}=\widetilde{a}_{v}^{\prime}\widetilde{\varphi}
and
S_{v}\widetilde{\varphi}=\widetilde{d}_{v}^{\prime}\widetilde{\varphi}
for some
\widetilde{a}_{v}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{O}}
lifting
a_{v}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{d}_{v}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{O}}
lifting
d_{v}^{\prime}
.

Letting
\Pi\in\mathcal{C}_{\vec{k}}^{B}
denote the (necessarily cuspidal) automorphic representation generated by

\widetilde{\varphi}
, we have that
\Pi_{w}^{U_{0}(w)_{w}}\neq 0
,
but
\Pi_{w}^{U_{w}}=0
, from which it follows that
\Pi_{w}
is a discrete series representation of
B_{w}^{\times}\cong\operatorname{GL}_{2}(F_{w})
. Theorem
2.3.1
therefore implies the existence of a cuspidal automorphic representation
\Pi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{\vec{k}}^{B^{\prime}}
such that
\Pi^{\prime}_{v}\cong\Pi_{v}
for all finite
v\neq w
, where
B^{\prime}
is a quaternion algebra over
F
such that
\Sigma^{B^{\prime}}=\Sigma^{B}\cup\{w\}-\{\sigma_{0}\}
for some archimedean place
\sigma_{0}
. This in turn yields an eigenform
\widetilde{\varphi}^{\prime}\in M_{\vec{k}}^{B^{\prime}}(U^{\prime},{\mathcal{O}})
, where
U^{\prime}
contains
{\mathcal{O}}_{B^{\prime},p}^{\times}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{B^{\prime},v}^{\times}
for all
v\not\in Q^{\prime}
, with
T_{v}\widetilde{\varphi}^{\prime}=\widetilde{a}_{v}^{\prime}\widetilde{\varphi}^{\prime}
and
S_{v}\widetilde{\varphi}^{\prime}=\widetilde{d}_{v}^{\prime}\widetilde{\varphi}^{\prime}
for all such
v
. Reducing mod
\varpi
thus yields an eigenform
\varphi^{\prime}\in M^{B^{\prime}}_{\vec{k}}(U^{\prime},E)
such that
T_{v}{\varphi}^{\prime}={a}_{v}^{\prime}{\varphi}^{\prime}
and
S_{v}{\varphi}^{\prime}={d}_{v}^{\prime}{\varphi}^{\prime}
for all
v\not\in Q^{\prime}
, and finally applying
e_{\xi}
yields the desired eigenform
f^{\prime}\in M^{B^{\prime}}_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U^{\prime},E)
such that
T_{v}f^{\prime}={a}_{v}f^{\prime}
and
S_{v}f^{\prime}={d}_{v}f^{\prime}
for all
v\not\in Q^{\prime}
.

(2) Now let
B
,
U
and
Q
be as in Definition
6.5.1
, but with
B
indefinite, so there exists a non-zero
f\in M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
for some sufficiently small
U
of level prime to
p
and sufficiently large
Q
such that
T_{v}f=a_{v}f
and
S_{v}f=d_{v}f
for all
v\not\in Q
, where

a_{v}={\operatorname{tr}\,}(\rho({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v}))
and
d_{v}=\mathrm{Nm}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(v)^{-1}\det(\rho({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v}))
.

Again choose
\xi:({\mathbb{A}}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})^{\times}\to\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times}
such that
\xi(\alpha)=\alpha^{\vec{m}+\vec{k}/2}
for all
\alpha\in{\mathcal{O}}_{F,(p),+}^{\times}
(and shrink
U
and enlarge
Q
and
E
), so that applying
e_{\xi}
yields
\varphi\in M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,E)
such that
T_{v}\varphi=a_{v}^{\prime}\varphi
and
S_{v}\varphi=d_{v}^{\prime}\varphi
for all
v\not\in Q
, where
a_{v}^{\prime}=\xi(\varpi_{v})^{-1}a_{v}
and
d_{v}^{\prime}=\xi(\varpi_{v})^{-2}d_{v}
.

The next step is to lift
\varphi
to an eigenform in
M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,{\mathcal{O}})
. To that end, let
\mathbb{T}
denote the
polynomial ring over
{\mathcal{O}}
in the variables
T_{v}
and
S_{v}
for
v\notin Q
, and
{\mathfrak{m}}
the kernel of the homomorphism
\mathbb{T}\to E
defined by
T_{v}\mapsto a_{v}^{\prime}
and
S_{v}\mapsto d_{v}^{\prime}
for
v\not\in Q
. We claim that the homomorphism
M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,{\mathcal{O}})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,E)_{{\mathfrak{m}}}
induced by reduction mod
\varpi
is surjective. Indeed from the long exact cohomology sequence

H^{1}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},{\mathcal{O}}})\to H^{1}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},E})\to H^{2}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},{\mathcal{O}}})\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}H^{2}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},{\mathcal{O}}})\to H^{2}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},E})

and Nakayama’s Lemma, we see that it suffices to prove that

H^{2}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},E})_{{\mathfrak{m}}}=0
.
Applying
e_{\xi}:H^{2}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}H^{2}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})
, we are reduced to proving that
H^{2}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q}}=0
, which was already established in the proof of Lemma
6.5.3
.
Similarly, we see that
H^{0}(Y_{U}^{B},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},E})_{{\mathfrak{m}}}=0
, and it follows that the sequence

0\longrightarrow M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,{\mathcal{O}})_{{\mathfrak{m}}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,{\mathcal{O}})_{{\mathfrak{m}}}\longrightarrow M_{\vec{k}}(U,E)_{{\mathfrak{m}}}\longrightarrow 0

is exact.

We have now shown that
M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,{\mathcal{O}})_{{\mathfrak{m}}}
is non-zero and torsion-free, so the usual argument gives an eigenform
\widetilde{\varphi}\in M^{B}_{\vec{k}}(U,K)
(after enlarging
K
if necessary) such that
T_{v}(\widetilde{\varphi})=\widetilde{a}_{v}^{\prime}\widetilde{\varphi}
and
S_{v}(\widetilde{\varphi})=\widetilde{d}_{v}^{\prime}\widetilde{\varphi}
for all
v\not\in Q
, where
\widetilde{a}_{v}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{O}}
lifts
a_{v}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{d}_{v}^{\prime}
lifts
d_{v}^{\prime}
. This in turns yields a cuspidal automorphic representation
\Pi\in\mathcal{C}_{\vec{k}}^{B}
, to which we can apply Theorem
2.3.1
to obtain an eigenform
\widetilde{\varphi}^{\prime}\in M_{\vec{k}}(U,K)
with the same eigenvalues as
\widetilde{\varphi}
for
T_{v}
and
S_{v}
for all
v\not\in Q
. Finally, the usual argument of reducing mod
\varpi
and applying
e_{\xi}
yields an eigenform
f^{\prime}\in M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,E)
with the same eigenvalues as
f
.
∎

Remark 6.5.6
.

As we will recall below (Corollary
6.5.10
), the converse to (1) in the preceding theorem follows from the main result of
[
GLS15
]
under a Taylor–Wiles hypothesis on
\rho
.

Alternatively, one can try to argue more directly along the lines of our proof of (1) above. However this requires an analogue of Ihara’s Lemma in the context of Shimura curves over
F
, for which the strongest results to date are due to Manning and Shotton
[
MS21
]
, which also require a Taylor–Wiles hypothesis on
\rho
.

Finally we remark that the argument we gave for part (2) of the theorem can be easily adapted to give a more direct proof that definite modularity of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
implies geometric modularity of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
.

Corollary 6.5.7
.

Suppose that
\pi
is as in the statement of Theorem
5.2.1
and that
\rho:=\overline{\rho}_{\pi}
is irreducible. Then
\rho
is geometrically modular of some weight
(\vec{\ell},\vec{n})
with
\vec{\ell}\in[2,p+1]^{\Sigma}
such that
\ell_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}=2
for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
,
i\in\mathbb{Z}/f_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbb{Z}
and
1\leq j\leq e_{\mathfrak{p}}-1
.

Proof.

Suppose first that
[F:\mathbb{Q}]
is odd, and let
B
be a quaternion algebra over
F
which is unramified at all finite places and exactly one archimedean place of
F
. By the Jacquet–Langlands correspondence, there is an irreducible subrepresentation
\Pi
of
|\det|^{w/2}\mathcal{S}_{\vec{k}}

such that
\Pi_{v}\cong\pi_{v}
for all finite primes
v
of
F
. For some sufficiently small open compact
U^{\prime}=U^{\prime}_{p}U^{\prime p}\subset B_{\mathbf{f}}^{\times}
, we have that
T_{v}
(resp.
\mathrm{Nm}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(v)S_{v}
) acts as
{\operatorname{tr}\,}(\rho_{\pi}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v}))
(resp.
\det(\rho_{\pi}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v}))
) on
\Pi^{U^{\prime}}
for all
v
not in some finite set of non-archimedean places
Q
of
F
, from which it follows that

{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q}
is in the support of

M^{B}_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U^{\prime},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})=H^{1}(Y^{B}_{U^{\prime}},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}),

where
m_{\sigma}=-(w+k_{\sigma})/2
for
\sigma\in\Sigma
.

Letting
U={\mathcal{O}}_{B,p}^{\times}U_{p}^{\prime}
, the projection
\varrho:Y_{U^{\prime}}^{B}\to Y_{U}^{B}
is étale (for sufficiently small
U_{p}^{\prime}
), and
\varrho_{*}\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
is isomorphic to the locally constant sheaf
\mathcal{T}
on
Y^{B}_{U}
associated to the smooth representation
T={\operatorname{Ind}\,}_{U_{p}^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{B,p}^{\times}}D_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{B,p}^{\times}
. We thus obtain an isomorphism
H^{1}(Y^{B}_{U^{\prime}},\mathcal{D}_{\vec{k},\vec{m},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})\cong H^{1}(Y^{B}_{U},\mathcal{T})

of
\mathbb{T}^{Q}
-modules, and (the easier direction of) the proof of Lemma
6.5.3
then implies that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}^{Q}
is in the support of
M_{\vec{\ell},\vec{n}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
for some Jordan–Hölder factor
D_{\vec{\ell},\vec{n},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
of
T
.

It follows that
\rho
is algebraically modular of some weight
(\vec{\ell},\vec{n})
as in the statement of the corollary. Theorem
6.5.5
(2) now implies that
\rho
is geometrically modular of such a weight.

The proof in the case that
[F:\mathbb{Q}]
is even is similar, but using forms on a definite quaternion algebra over
F
and parts (1) and (2) of the theorem.
∎

Corollary 6.5.8
.

Suppose that
\rho
is irreducible and geometrically modular of some weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
. Then it is geometrically modular for some weight
(\vec{\ell},\vec{n})
as in the conclusion of Corollary
6.5.7
.

Proof.

From the construction of the Galois representation
\rho_{f}
associated to an eigenform
f\in M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
, we have that
\rho_{f}\cong{\overline{\rho}}_{\pi}
for some
\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{\vec{2}}
. Applying Corollary
6.5.7
therefore gives the desired conclusion.
∎

In view of Theorem
6.5.5
, one can obtain results towards Conjecture
6.3.2
using ones on the algebraic Serre weight conjecture, which was proved by Gee–Liu–Savitt
[
GLS15
]
(combined with work of Gee–Kisin
[
GK19
]
or Newton
[
New14
]
) under a Taylor–Wiles hypothesis. We recall the statement, or more precisely a variant which is immediate from Theorems 4.2.1 and 6.1.8 of
[
GLS15
]
and the fact that if
\rho
is geometrically modular of some weight, then it is algebraically modular of some weight:

Theorem 6.5.9

(Gee–Liu–Savitt)
.

Suppose that
\rho
is irreducible and geometrically modular of some weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
. Suppose further that
p>2
,
\rho|_{F(\zeta_{p})}
is irreducible, and if
p=5
, then the projective image of
\rho|_{F(\zeta_{5})}
is not isomorphic to
A_{5}
. If
(\vec{\ell},\vec{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}_{\geq 2}\times\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
is such that
D^{\prime}_{\vec{\ell},\vec{n},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}}
is irreducible, then the following are equivalent:

(1)

\rho
is definitely modular of weight
(\vec{\ell},\vec{n})
;

(2)

\rho
is algebraically modular of weight
(\vec{\ell},\vec{n})
;

(3)

\rho|_{G_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
has a crystalline lift of weight
(\vec{\ell}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{n}_{\mathfrak{p}})
for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
.

Combined with Lemma
6.5.3
, this implies:

Corollary 6.5.10
.

Suppose that
p>2
,
(\vec{k},\vec{m})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}_{\geq 2}\times\Sigma
, and
\rho:G_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
is such that
\rho|_{F(\zeta_{p})}
is irreducible, and if
p=5
, then the projective image of
\rho|_{F(\zeta_{5})}
is not isomorphic to
A_{5}
. Then
\rho
is definitely modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
if and only if

\rho
is algebraically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
.

6.6.
Ordinary modularity

Suppose now that
\vec{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}^{\Sigma}
, so
the Hecke operator
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
on
M_{\vec{k},-\vec{1}}(U,E)
is defined for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
. Since the operators
T_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
commute with each other as well as the
T_{v}
and
S_{v}
for
v\not\in Q
, it follows that if
\rho\sim\rho_{f}
for some
eigenform
f\in M_{\vec{k},-\vec{1}}(U,E)
as in the statement of Theorem
5.2.1
, then we may in fact take
f
to be an eigenform for the
T_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
as well.

Recall from Theorem
5.3.1
that if
f
is ordinary at a prime
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
in the sense that
T_{{\mathfrak{p}}}f=a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}f
for some
a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\in E^{\times}
, then so is the associated local Galois representation
\rho_{f}|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
in the sense that it has an unramified subrepresentation (with our conventions), provided
k_{\sigma}>1
for some
\sigma\in S_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
.

On the other hand, if
k_{\sigma}=1
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
, then one direction of Conjecture
6.3.2
amounts to the well-known expectation that
\rho_{f}
be unramified at
{\mathfrak{p}}
.
Using the argument of
[
DDW24
, Prop. 2.5]
, we at least have
the following:

Proposition 6.6.1
.

Suppose that
f\in M_{\vec{k},-\vec{1}}(U,E)
is an eigenform (as in Theorem
5.2.1
), and moreover that
T_{\mathfrak{p}}f=a_{\mathfrak{p}}f
for some
a_{\mathfrak{p}}\in E
. Then
\rho_{f}|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\mathrm{ss}}
is unramified and
a_{\mathfrak{p}}(f)={\operatorname{tr}\,}(\rho_{f}(\gamma))
for any

\gamma
lifting
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
to
G_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
.

Proof.

We first note that
\det(\rho_{f})
is unramified at

{\mathfrak{p}}
, and moreover that

\det(\rho_{f}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{\mathfrak{p}}))=\delta\epsilon\varpi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\vec{k}-\vec{1}}\in E^{\times},

where
\delta
is the eigenvalue of
S_{\varpi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
on
f
and
\epsilon
is the reduction of
p^{f_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathrm{Nm}(\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}})^{-1}
(see §
3.4
).

Letting
\vec{k}^{\prime}=\vec{k}+\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\vec{h}_{\sigma}
and relating the actions of
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
under the “doubling map”

M_{\vec{k},-\vec{1}}(U,E)^{2}\longrightarrow M_{\vec{k}^{\prime},-\vec{1}}(U,E)

as in
[
DDW24
, Prop. 2.5]
, one deduces the existence of an eigenform
f_{\alpha}\in M_{\vec{k}^{\prime},-\vec{1}}(U,E)
such that
T_{\mathfrak{p}}f_{\alpha}=\alpha f_{\alpha}
and
\rho_{f_{\alpha}}\sim\rho_{f}
for each root
\alpha
of the polynomial

X^{2}-a_{\mathfrak{p}}(f)X+\det(\rho_{f}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{\mathfrak{p}})).

It then follows from Theorem
5.3.1
that
\rho_{f}

has a non-trivial unramified subrepresentation
L_{\alpha}
on which

{\operatorname{Frob}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
acts as
\alpha
.

If the polynomial above has distinct roots
\alpha\neq\alpha^{\prime}
, then it follows that
\rho|_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
is isomorphic to
L_{\alpha}\oplus L_{\alpha^{\prime}}
. Therefore
\rho_{f}
is unramified at
{\mathfrak{p}}
and

{\operatorname{tr}\,}(\rho_{f}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}))=\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}=a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}.

On the other hand, if the polynomial factors as
(X-\alpha)^{2}
, then
\det(\rho_{f})
is the unramified character sending
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
to
\alpha^{2}
. Therefore the quotient of
\rho_{f}|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
by
L_{\alpha}
is also isomorphic to
L_{\alpha}
, and
\rho|_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\mathrm{ss}}
is isomorphic to
L_{\alpha}\oplus L_{\alpha}
, again giving the desired conclusion.
∎

Remark 6.6.2
.

Note that for
f
as in the proposition, it is immediate that
\rho_{f}
is unramified at
{\mathfrak{p}}
if the polynomial in the proof has distinct roots (or if
\rho_{f}
itself is reducible). If it has repeated roots (and
\rho_{f}
is irreducible), then the unramifiedness is proved by De Maria (
[
DM20
, Thm. D]
) under the additional assumption that the
k_{\sigma}
are odd (for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
).

In view of the proposition, if
k_{\sigma}=1
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
, then we view any
T_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
-eigenform
f\in M_{\vec{k},-\vec{1}}(U,E)
as being ordinary at
{\mathfrak{p}}
.

We extend the notions of ordinariness to more general weights

(\vec{k},\vec{m})
with
\vec{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}^{\Sigma}
by twisting by characters as in §
6.4
. Suppose that
f\in M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,E)
is an eigenform
for
T_{v}
and
S_{v}
for all
v\not\in Q

(as in Theorem
5.2.1
). Enlarging
E
if necessary, let
\xi:\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}/F^{\times}F_{\infty,+}^{\times}\to E^{\times}
be a continuous character such that
\xi(u_{p})=\overline{u}_{p}^{\vec{m}+\vec{1}}
for all
u\in{\mathcal{O}}_{F,p}^{\times}
, so its restriction to
(\mathbb{A}_{F,\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})^{\times}
is as in §
3.5
with
\vec{\ell}=-\vec{m}-\vec{1}
.
Shrinking
U
and enlarging
Q
as necessary, we have that

e_{\xi}f\in M_{\vec{k},-\vec{1}}(U,E)
is an eigenform for
T_{v}
and
S_{v}
for all
v\not\in Q
. We say that
f
is
{\mathfrak{p}}
-ordinary
if either
k_{\sigma}=1
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
or
T_{\mathfrak{p}}(e_{\xi}f)=a_{{\mathfrak{p}},\xi}e_{\xi}f
for some
a_{{\mathfrak{p}},\xi}=a_{{\mathfrak{p}},\xi}(f)\in E^{\times}
. If
\xi_{1}
and
\xi_{2}
are two such characters, then

T_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(e_{\xi_{2}}f)=T_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(e_{\mu}e_{\xi_{1}}f)=\mu(\varpi_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{p}}})e_{\mu}T_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(e_{\xi_{1}}f)

where
\mu=\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}
.
It follows that the notion is independent of the choice of
\xi
,
and that if
f
is
{\mathfrak{p}}
-ordinary, then the
a_{{\mathfrak{p}},\xi}
as
\xi
varies are related by the formula

a_{{\mathfrak{p}},\xi_{2}}=\mu({\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}})a_{{\mathfrak{p}},\xi_{1}}
, where we have written
\mu
also for the character
G_{F}\to E^{\times}
to which it corresponds via class field theory.

Definition 6.6.3
.

Suppose that
S
is a subset of
S_{p}
,
\vec{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}^{\Sigma}
and
\vec{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
. We say that a continuous, irreducible
\rho:G_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
is

S
-ordinarily geometrically modular
if
\rho
arises from some
f\in M_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,E)
(as in Theorem
5.2.1
) which is
{\mathfrak{p}}
-ordinary for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S
.

We may similarly define ordinarily definite and algebraic modularity. Recall that for
\vec{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}^{\Sigma}
,

{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
and
B
and
U
as in Definition
6.5.1
,
the Hecke operator
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
is defined on
M_{\vec{k},-\vec{1}}^{B}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
(see §
3.6
,
3.7
). Recall also that for arbitrary
\vec{m}
, we have (after possibly shrinking
U
) isomorphisms

e_{\xi}:M^{B}_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}M^{B}_{\vec{k},-\vec{1}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})

associated to characters
\xi:\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}/F^{\times}F_{\infty,+}^{\times}\to\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times}
such that
\xi(u_{p})=\overline{u}_{p}^{\vec{m}+\vec{1}}
for all
u_{p}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{F,p}^{\times}
.

Definition 6.6.4
.

Suppose that
S
is a subset of
S_{p}
,
\vec{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}^{\Sigma}
and
\vec{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
. We say that a continuous, irreducible
\rho:G_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
is

S
-ordinarily definitely
(resp.
algebraically
)
modular
if there exists a non-zero
\varphi\in M^{B}_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})[{\mathfrak{m}}^{Q}_{\rho}]
for some
B
,
U
and
Q
as in Definition
6.5.1
, such that
B
is totally definite (resp. unramified at exactly one archimedean place) and for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S
, we have

T_{\mathfrak{p}}(e_{\xi}\varphi)=a_{{\mathfrak{p}},\xi}e_{\xi}\varphi

for some
a_{{\mathfrak{p}},\xi}=a_{{\mathfrak{p}},\xi}(\varphi)\in\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times}
and character
\xi:\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}/F^{\times}F_{\infty,+}^{\times}\to\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times}
as above.

Just as for ordinary geometric modularity, if the condition on
e_{\xi}\varphi
in the definition holds for some character
\xi
such that
\xi(u_{p})=\overline{u}_{p}^{\vec{m}+\vec{1}}
for all
u_{p}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{F,p}^{\times}
above, then in fact the condition holds for all such
\xi
. Furthermore, the
a_{{\mathfrak{p}},\xi}
for varying
\xi
are related in the same way as for ordinarily geometric modularity.

The analogue of Theorem
5.3.1
holds in the setting for definite or algebraic modularity. Furthermore, we can extend the description of the local behavior at
{\mathfrak{p}}
to all
{\mathfrak{p}}
-ordinarily modular Galois representations as follows (writing
{\mathfrak{p}}
instead of
\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}
).

Theorem 6.6.5
.

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
,
\vec{k},\vec{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
, with
k_{\sigma}\geq 1
(resp.
\geq 2
) for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
, and that
\rho:G_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
is irreducible and
{\mathfrak{p}}
-ordinarily geometrically (resp. definitely or algebraically) modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
.
Then

\rho|_{G_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}\sim\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\chi_{2}&*\\
0&\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}^{-1}\chi_{1}\end{array}\right),

for some characters
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}:G_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\to\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times}
, where
\chi_{2}
corresponds via class field theory to the character
F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}\to\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times}
defined by

x\mapsto\xi^{-1}(x)a_{{\mathfrak{p}},\xi}^{v_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(x)}
for any
\xi
and
a_{{\mathfrak{p}},\xi}
as in Definition
6.6.3
(resp.
6.6.4
), unless
k_{\sigma}=1
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
, in which case the same conclusion holds with
a_{{\mathfrak{p}},\xi}
replaced by any root of the polynomial

X^{2}-a_{{\mathfrak{p}},\xi}X+(\chi^{2}\det(\rho))({\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}),

where
\chi
corresponds via class field theory to
\xi
(so
\chi^{2}\det(\rho)
is unramified at
{\mathfrak{p}}
).

Proof.

Consider first the case of
{\mathfrak{p}}
-ordinarily geometric modularity, and let
f
and
\xi
be as in Definition
6.6.3
. Since
\rho_{f}\sim\chi^{-1}\rho_{e_{\xi}f}
(where
\chi
corresponds to
\xi
), we can replace
f
by
e_{\xi}f
and reduce to the case
\vec{m}=-\vec{1}
and
\xi=1
. The assertions are then given by Theorem
5.3.1
and Proposition
6.6.1
.

Consider now the case of
{\mathfrak{p}}
-ordinarily definite or algebraic modularity, and let
\varphi
and
\xi
be as in Definition
6.6.4
. Since
e_{\xi}\varphi\in M^{B}_{\vec{k},-\vec{1}}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})[{\mathfrak{m}}^{Q}_{\chi\otimes\rho}]
, we can again reduce to the case
\vec{m}=-\vec{1}
and
\xi=1
. An inspection of the proof of Theorem
5.3.1
(starting from the introduction of the representation
L_{\chi}
) shows that this is precisely the input required in order to obtain the desired conclusion, except that it is assumed at that point
19
19

19

The formula
\varepsilon\circ\Delta=\delta\circ\varepsilon
in the proof fails if
k_{\sigma}=2
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
.
that
k_{\sigma}>2
for some
\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
. However if
k_{\sigma}=2
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
, then one can a give a similar (and much simpler) argument by making the following changes:

Firstly, in the setup, replace the representation
L_{\chi}
by the trivial representation
E
,
\varepsilon
by the identity,
U_{B,0}
and
U_{B,1}
by
U_{B}
and
\pi
by the identity.

One can then skip directly to the introduction of the subgroup
U_{B,1}^{\prime}
in the proof. The formulas describing
T_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
on the kernel of (
14
) in the algebraic case
20
20

20

Note also that whether we are in the algebraic and definite case is no longer determined by whether the degree
d=[F:\mathbb{Q}]
is even or odd.
and on
I_{\vec{2}}^{B}(U_{B,1}^{\prime},{\mathbb{C}})
in the definite case are no longer valid (the factor of
p^{f_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
being replaced by
p^{f_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}+1
). However if
\varphi
is in the kernel of (
14
) or
\widetilde{\varphi}\in I_{\vec{2}}^{B}(U_{B,1}^{\prime},{\mathbb{C}})
, then the proof of Theorem
5.2.1
gives that
\rho\sim\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}^{-1}\psi\oplus\psi
for some character
\psi
, contradicting our assumption here that
\rho
is irreducible.

Finally we obtain a cuspidal automorphic representation
\pi\in\mathcal{C}_{\vec{2}}
such that
\rho\sim{\overline{\rho}}_{\pi}
and
\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
is an unramified principal series
I(\psi_{1}|\cdot|^{1/2},\psi_{2}|\cdot|^{1/2})
such that
\psi_{1}(\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}})
and
\psi_{2}(\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}})
are the roots of

X^{2}-\widetilde{a}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}X+\widetilde{d}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}p^{f_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}

for some lift
\widetilde{a}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
of
a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
and root of unity
\widetilde{d}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
(both viewed in
{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}\cap{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}\subset\mathbb{C}
). We may therefore assume that
\psi_{2}(\varpi_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
is a lift of
a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
and conclude exactly as in the proof of Theorem
5.3.1
.
∎

We conjecture the following converse to Theorem
6.6.5
:

Conjecture 6.6.6
.

Suppose that

\rho:G_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
is irreducible,
S\subset S_{p}
and

(\vec{k},\vec{m})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}^{\Sigma}\times\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
. Suppose that
\rho|_{G_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
has a crystalline lift of weight
(k_{\sigma},m_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
, and moreover that for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S
, we have

\rho|_{G_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}\sim\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\chi_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}&*\\
0&\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}^{-1}\chi_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}\end{array}\right)

for some characters
\chi_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}
,
\chi_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}
such that
\chi_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}|_{I_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
corresponds via class field theory to the character
{\mathcal{O}}_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\times}\to\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times}
defined by
\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\overline{\sigma}^{-1-m_{\sigma}}
. Then
\rho
is
S
-ordinarily geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
, and furthermore is
S
-ordinarily definitely and algebraically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
if
\vec{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}_{\geq 2}
.

Remark 6.6.7
.

Note that the hypotheses on
\rho|_{G_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
imply also that the characters
\chi_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}|_{I_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
correspond to
\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\overline{\sigma}^{1-k_{\sigma}-m_{\sigma}}
for
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S
. Conversely, if

\rho|_{G_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
has this form for such characters
\chi_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}
,
\chi_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}
, then it necessarily has a crystalline lift of weight
(k_{\sigma},m_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
, provided
k_{\sigma}\geq 2
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
and some
k_{\sigma}>2
.

We expect that the proof of Theorem
6.5.9
can be modified (by imposing an ordinariness condition in the relevant local deformation rings) to establish the preceding conjecture in the definite and algebraic settings under the additional hypotheses of that theorem. However, we have not checked the details, so we content ourselves with the following result, which can be deduced more directly from results of Gee–Kisin
[
GK19
]
, and will suffice for the purpose of the intended applications to the ramified quadratic case in the next section.

Theorem 6.6.8
.

Suppose that
\rho
is irreducible and geometrically modular of some weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
. Suppose further that
p>2
,
\rho|_{F(\zeta_{p})}
is irreducible, and if
p=5
, then the projective image of
\rho|_{F(\zeta_{5})}
is not isomorphic to
A_{5}
. Let
(\vec{\ell},\vec{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}_{\geq 2}\times\mathbb{Z}^{\Sigma}
be such that
D^{\prime}_{\vec{\ell},\vec{n}}
is irreducible and for each
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
,

•

\max_{1\leq j\leq e_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\ell_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}<p+1
for some
i\in\mathbb{Z}/f_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathbb{Z}
;

•

\rho|_{G_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
has a crystalline lift of weight
(\vec{\ell}_{\mathfrak{p}},\vec{n}_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
;

•

\rho|_{G_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}\sim\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\chi_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}&*\\
0&\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}^{-1}\chi_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}\end{array}\right)

for some characters
\chi_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}
,
\chi_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}
such that
\chi_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}|_{I_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
corresponds to the character
{\mathcal{O}}_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\times}\to\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}^{\times}
defined by
\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\overline{\sigma}^{-1-n_{\sigma}}
.

Then
\rho
is
S_{p}
-ordinarily definitely and algebraically modular of weight
(\vec{\ell},\vec{n})
.

Proof.

Firstly, we can reduce to the case
\vec{n}=-\vec{1}
by twisting.

Note that the hypothesis that
\rho|_{G_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
has a crystalline lift of weight
(\ell_{\sigma},n_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
implies that
\chi_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}|_{I_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
corresponds to
\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\overline{\sigma}^{2-\ell_{\sigma}}
. Recall also that the hypothesis that
D^{\prime}_{\vec{\ell},\vec{n}}
is irreducible means that
2\leq\ell_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}\leq p+1
for all
i=1,\ldots,e_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}/f_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathbb{Z}
, and that
\ell_{{\mathfrak{p}},i,j}>2
for at most one
j
for each
i
.
Let
S_{0}
denote the set of primes in
S_{p}
such that

k_{\sigma}\neq 2
for some
\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
.

For each
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
, let
\tau_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
denote the inertia type
1\oplus\xi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
, where
\xi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}:I_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
(for some sufficiently large
{\mathcal{O}}
) is the Teichmüller lift of
\chi_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}|_{I_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
.

As in the proof of Theorem
6.5.5
, let us also choose an auxiliary prime
w\not\in S_{p}
at which
\rho
is unramified and the conjugacy class of
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{w}
in
{\operatorname{Gal}\,}(L(\zeta_{p})/F)
is that of a complex conjugation, where
L
is the splitting field of
\rho
.

By
[
GK19
, Lemma 4.2.5]
(applied to
\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}\otimes\rho
), there is an automorphic representation
\pi\in\mathcal{C}_{\vec{2}}
such that

•

\overline{\rho}_{\pi}\sim\rho
;

•

\pi_{w}\sim\psi_{w}\otimes\mathrm{St}
for some unramified character
\psi_{w}
;

•

if
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
, then
\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
is principal series of the form
I(\psi_{1}|\cdot|^{1/2},\psi_{2}|\cdot|^{1/2})
where
\psi_{1}|_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}}
corresponds to
\xi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
and
\psi_{2}
is unramified,

•

if
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
, then
\rho|_{G_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}\sim\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\widetilde{\chi}_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}&*\\
0&\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}^{-1}\widetilde{\chi}_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}\end{array}\right)
, where
\widetilde{\chi}_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}
is an unramified lift of
\chi_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}
and
\widetilde{\chi}_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}
is a tamely ramified lift of
\chi_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}
(so
\widetilde{\chi}_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}|_{I_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}=\xi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
).

Since
\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
corresponds via local Langlands to
\mathrm{WD}(\rho_{\pi}|_{G_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}})\cong\widetilde{\chi}_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}\oplus\widetilde{\chi}_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}|\cdot|^{-1}
, we have
\psi_{1}=\widetilde{\chi}_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}|\cdot|^{-1}
and
\psi_{2}=\widetilde{\chi}_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}
, after possibly interchanging
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
if
{\mathfrak{p}}\not\in S_{0}
(conflating characters of
W_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
and
F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}
).

Let
B
denote the totally definite quaternion algebra over
F
ramified at either
S_{\infty}
or
S_{\infty}\cup\{w\}
, according to whether
d
is even or odd. Then by Theorem
2.3.1
, there is an automorphic representation
\Pi\in\mathcal{C}_{\vec{2}}^{B}
such that
\pi_{v}\cong\Pi_{v}
for all finite places
v\neq w
.

For
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
, define
U_{{\mathfrak{p}},0}
(resp.
U_{{\mathfrak{p}},1}
) to be
{\mathcal{O}}_{B,{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}
to be the open compact subgroup of
{\mathcal{O}}_{B,{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}
corresponding to

U_{0}({\mathfrak{p}})_{{\mathfrak{p}}}=\left\{\left.\,{\mbox{\scriptsize$\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{{a}}&{{b}}\\
{{c}}&{{d}}\end{array}\right)$}}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}})\,\right|\,c\in{\mathfrak{p}}{\mathcal{O}}_{F,{\mathfrak{p}}}\,\right\}

(resp.
U_{1}({\mathfrak{p}})_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
); otherwise let
U_{{\mathfrak{p}},0}=U_{{\mathfrak{p}},1}={\mathcal{O}}_{B,{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}
. Let
U_{1}\subset U_{0}\subset U
be open compact subgroups of
B_{\mathbf{f}}^{\times}
of the form

U_{1,p}U^{p}\subset U_{0,p}U^{p}\subset U_{p}U^{p},

where
U_{i,p}=\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}}U_{i,{\mathfrak{p}}}
for
i=0,1
,
U_{p}={\mathcal{O}}_{B,p}^{\times}
, and (the same)
U^{p}\subset(B_{\mathbf{f}}^{(p)})^{\times}
is sufficiently small that
\Pi^{U_{1}}\neq 0
(in addition to the usual sense).

For a sufficiently large finite set of primes of
Q
containing
S_{p}
, we have
T_{v}=\widetilde{a}_{v}
and
S_{v}=\widetilde{d}_{v}
on
\Pi^{U_{1}}
for all
v\not\in Q
, where
\widetilde{a}_{v}
is a lift of
{\operatorname{tr}\,}(\rho({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v}))
and
\widetilde{d}_{v}
is a lift of
\mathrm{Nm}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(v)^{-1}\det(\rho({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v}))
. Furthermore, for
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{0}
, we have
T_{{\mathfrak{p}}}=\widetilde{a}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
on
\Pi^{U_{1}}
, where
\widetilde{a}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
is a lift of
\chi_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
(more precisely,
\widetilde{\chi}_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
or
\widetilde{\chi}_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}})+p^{f_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\widetilde{\chi}_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
, according to whether or not
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{0}
). Furthermore for each
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{0}
,
U_{0,{\mathfrak{p}}}/U_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}\cong\mathbb{F}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}
acts on
\Pi^{U_{1}}
via the Teichmüller lift of
\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\overline{\sigma}^{2-\ell_{\sigma}}
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{Q}
denote the polynomial ring over
{\mathcal{O}}
in the variables
T_{v}
and
S_{v}
for
v\not\in Q
, and
T_{\mathfrak{p}}
for
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
, and let
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{m}}}
denote the kernel of the homomorphism
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{Q}\to E
defined by

\begin{array}[]{c}T_{v}\mapsto{\operatorname{tr}\,}(\rho({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v}))\quad\mbox{and}\quad S_{v}\mapsto\mathrm{Nm}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(v)^{-1}\det(\rho({\operatorname{Frob}}_{v})),\quad\mbox{for $v\not\in Q$;}\\
T_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\mapsto\chi_{2,{\mathfrak{p}}}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}),\quad\mbox{for ${\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}$.}\end{array}

Let
\chi=\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{0}}\overline{\sigma}^{\ell_{\sigma}-2}
and
\widetilde{\chi}
its Teichmüller lift, viewed as characters of
U_{0,p}/U_{1,p}\cong\mathbb{F}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}
, inflated to characters of
U_{0,p}
, and consider the action of
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{Q}
on

M^{B}(U_{0},{\mathcal{O}}(\widetilde{\chi}))=(M^{B}(U_{1},{\mathcal{O}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{O}}(\widetilde{\chi}))^{U_{0}}.

Since the surjective map

M^{B}(U_{1},{\mathcal{O}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathbb{C}=M^{B}(U_{1},\mathbb{C})\twoheadrightarrow\Pi^{U_{1}}

is compatible with the action of
U_{0}
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}
, it follows that there is an eigenform in
M^{B}(U_{0},{\mathcal{O}}(\widetilde{\chi}))
for
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{Q}
on which
T_{v}
acts as
\widetilde{a}_{v}
and
S_{v}
as
\widetilde{d}_{v}
for
v\not\in Q
, and
T_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
as
\widetilde{a}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
for
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
. Therefore the maximal ideal
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{m}}}^{Q}
is in the support of
M^{B}(U_{0},{\mathcal{O}}(\widetilde{\chi}))
, and hence in that of

M^{B}(U_{0},E(\chi))
.

Now let
T=\mathrm{Coind}_{U_{0,p}}^{U_{p}}E(\chi)
.
As in the proof of Theorem
5.3.1
, we have a
U_{p}
-equivariant homomorphism

\varepsilon:D^{\prime}_{\vec{\ell},-\vec{1}}\to T
;
in this case it is even injective as a consequence of the irreducibility of
D^{\prime}_{\vec{\ell},-\vec{1}}
. Furthermore, we have an endomorphism
T_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
on
M^{B}(U,T)
for each
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p}
which is compatible with the resulting maps

M^{B}_{\vec{\ell},-\vec{1}}(U,E)\longrightarrow M^{B}(U,T)\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}M^{B}(U_{0},E(\chi)).

We claim that
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{m}}}_{\rho}
is not in the support of the cokernel. In order to prove this, write

S_{0}=\{{\mathfrak{p}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathfrak{p}}_{r}\}
,
D_{\vec{\ell},-\vec{1}}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{r}D_{i}
where
D_{i}=\bigotimes_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}}}\operatorname{Sym}^{\ell_{\sigma}-2}E^{2}
,
T=\bigotimes_{i}T_{i}
where
T_{i}={\operatorname{Coind}\,}_{U_{0,{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{U_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\chi_{i}
and
\chi_{i}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}}}\overline{\sigma}^{\ell_{\sigma}-2}
, and factor
\varepsilon
as the composite of morphisms of the form

\varepsilon_{i}:(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{j-1}T_{i})\otimes(\bigotimes_{i=j}^{r}D_{i})\longrightarrow(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{j}T_{i})\otimes(\bigotimes_{i=j+1}^{r}D_{i})

for
j=1,\ldots,r
(defined as the tensor product of the morphism
D^{\prime}_{j}\to T_{j}
with the identity in the other factors).
The argument in the proof of Theorem
5.3.1
then shows that
T_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}}
annihilates the cokernel of the map on cohomology induced by
\varepsilon_{i}
, from which it follows that
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{m}}}_{\rho}
is not in its support. Therefore
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{m}}}_{\rho}
is not in the support of the cokernel of the resulting composite morphism. It follows that
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{m}}}_{\rho}
is in the support of
M^{B}_{\vec{\ell},-\vec{1}}(U,E)
and hence that
M^{B}_{\vec{\ell},-\vec{1}}(U,E)[\widetilde{{\mathfrak{m}}}_{\rho}]\neq 0
, which implies that
\rho
is
S_{p}
-ordinarily definitely modular.

The proof of
S_{p}
-ordinarily algebraic modularity is exactly the same, starting instead with the quaternion algebra over
F
ramified at either
S_{\infty}\cup\{w\}-\{\sigma_{0}\}
or
S_{\infty}-\{\sigma_{0}\}
for some
\sigma_{0}\in\Sigma_{\infty}
.
∎

We will also make use of the following refinement of some of the implications in Theorem
6.5.5
:

Theorem 6.6.9
.

Suppose that
\rho
,
\vec{k}
and
\vec{m}
are as in Conjecture
6.5.2
, and let
S
be a subset of
S_{p}
. If
\rho
is
S
-ordinarily definitely or algebraically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
, then
\rho
is
S
-ordinarily geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k},\vec{m})
.

Proof.

Firstly, as usual we may twist to reduce to the case

\vec{m}=-\vec{1}
and suppose the existence of a non-zero
