\psi|_{I_{F_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}=\omega^{-m-2}
,
\chi|_{I_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}=\omega
and
c\in V_{\chi}
. In particular,
\rho
has a crystalline lift of weight
(\vec{k}^{\prime\prime},\vec{m})
, where
\vec{k}^{\prime\prime}=(2,p)
, and Theorem
6.6.8
implies that
\rho
is

{\mathfrak{p}}
-ordinarily algebraically modular of weight
(\vec{k}^{\prime\prime},\vec{m})
. Therefore
\rho
is also
{\mathfrak{p}}
-ordinarily geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k}^{\prime\prime},\vec{m})
by Theorem
6.6.9
. By Proposition
7.1.5
and Lemma
7.1.7
, it follows that
\rho
arises from a stabilized eigenform
f\in S_{\vec{k}^{\prime\prime},\vec{m}}(U_{1}({\mathfrak{n}}),E)
for some
{\mathfrak{n}}
not divisible by
{\mathfrak{p}}
, and moreover with
T_{{\mathfrak{p}}}f_{\xi}\neq 0
for all
\xi
as in Definition
7.1.4
.

Since
c\in V_{\chi}
, we see as in the proof of Theorem
7.4.1
that
\rho
also has a crystalline lift of weight
(\vec{k}^{\prime},\vec{m}^{\prime})
, where
\vec{k}^{\prime}=(2,3)
and
\vec{m}^{\prime}=\vec{m}-(0,1)
. Therefore Theorems
6.5.9
and
6.5.5
imply that
\rho
is geometrically modular of weight
(\vec{k}^{\prime},\vec{m}^{\prime})
. By Proposition
7.1.5
and Lemma
7.1.7
,
\rho
arises from a stabilized eigenform
f^{\prime}\in S_{\vec{k}^{\prime},\vec{m}}(U,E)
, where
U=U({\mathfrak{n}}^{\prime})
for some
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\prime}
not divisible by
{\mathfrak{p}}
. Furthermore another application of Lemma
7.1.7
allows us to replace
{\mathfrak{n}}
and
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\prime}
by a common multiple, and hence assume
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\prime}={\mathfrak{n}}
.

We claim that we can in fact take
f^{\prime}
to be strongly stabilized. Indeed this follows from Theorem
5.3.1
, unless
p=3
and
\rho|_{I_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}\sim 1\oplus\omega
. In that case, note that Theorem
4.2.1
implies that
H_{2}|\Theta(f)
, so
\rho
arises from a strongly stabilized
g\in S_{(2,5),\vec{m}^{\prime}}(U,E)
.
Similarly
H_{2}|\Theta(f^{\prime})
, so
\rho
arises from a strongly stabilized
g^{\prime}\in S_{(2,5),\vec{m}^{\prime\prime}}(U,E)
, where
\vec{m}^{\prime\prime}=\vec{m}-(0,2)
. It follows that
\Theta(g)=H_{1}H_{2}^{2}g^{\prime}
is divisible by
H_{2}
. Theorem
4.2.1
then implies that
g=H_{2}g^{\prime\prime}
for some
g^{\prime\prime}\in S_{(1,6),\vec{m}^{\prime}}(U,E)
, and Theorem
4.2.3
then implies
g^{\prime\prime}=H_{1}f^{\prime\prime}
, where now
f^{\prime\prime}\in S_{\vec{k}^{\prime},\vec{m}^{\prime}}(U,E)
is (up to scalar) a strongly stabilized eigenform giving rise to
\rho
. We can therefore replace
f^{\prime}
by
f^{\prime\prime}
to complete the proof of the claim.

Now write
\Theta^{p-2}f^{\prime}=G_{1}^{-1}G_{2}^{-1}H_{1}^{s_{1}}H_{2}^{s_{2}}f^{\prime\prime\prime}
, where
f^{\prime\prime\prime}\in S_{\vec{k}^{\prime\prime\prime},\vec{m}}(U,E)
is (up to scalar) a strongly stabilized eigenform not divisible by
H_{1}
or
H_{2}
. By Theorem
4.2.3
, we have

\vec{k}^{\prime\prime\prime}=(p,p+1)-a_{1}(-1,p)-a_{2}(1,-1)\in\Xi_{\min}^{+}

for some
a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Furthermore
\Theta^{p-1}f^{\prime}=G_{1}G_{2}H_{1}^{2}H_{2}^{p+1}
, so Theorem
4.2.1
implies that either
a_{2}\geq p+1
or
k_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}=p+1-pa_{1}+a_{2}
is divisible by
p
. Using that
\vec{k}^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\Xi_{\min}^{+}
, the first possibility implies that
a_{1}=2
and
a_{2}=p+1
, so
\vec{k}^{\prime\prime\prime}=(1,2)=\vec{k}
and we are done.

So suppose that
a_{2}<p+1
and
p|k_{2}^{\prime}
, i.e.,
a_{2}\equiv-1\bmod p
, so
a_{2}=p-1
. Again using that
\vec{k}^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\Xi_{\min}^{+}
, we find that
a_{1}=1
, so
\vec{k}^{\prime\prime\prime}=(2,p)=\vec{k}^{\prime\prime}
. Since
f
and
f^{\prime\prime\prime}
are both stabilized eigenforms in
S_{\vec{k}^{\prime\prime},\vec{m}}(U,E)
, but only
f^{\prime\prime\prime}
is strongly stabilized, we have
f-f^{\prime\prime\prime}
in
\ker(\Theta)
. Applying Theorem
4.2.2
now implies that
f-f^{\prime\prime\prime}=G_{1}^{t_{1}}G_{2}^{t_{2}}V_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(g^{\prime\prime\prime})
for some
t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
and eigenform
g^{\prime\prime\prime}\in S_{\vec{k},\vec{m}}(U,E)
giving rise to
\rho
, and we are done.
∎

Remark 7.4.7
.

Recall that
\rho|_{G_{F(\zeta_{p})}}
is irreducible unless
\rho
is induced from a character of
G_{L}
, where
L
is the quadratic extension of
F
contained in
F(\zeta_{p})
. From the shape of the local Galois representation, one sees easily that (under the other assumptions of Theorem
7.4.6
) this is only possible if
{\mathfrak{p}}
splits in
L
.

Finally, we record the following consequence used in the proof of Theorem
7.4.1
:

Corollary 7.4.8
.

Under the hypotheses of Theorem
7.4.6
, there exist an ideal
{\mathfrak{n}}
prime to
{\mathfrak{p}}
and strongly stabilized eigenform
f\in S_{(2,p),\vec{m}}(U_{1}({\mathfrak{n}}),E)
such that
\rho\sim\rho_{f}
.

This was in fact established in the course of the proof of Theorem
7.4.6
, but can also be deduced from the statement of the theorem by taking a linear combination of
G^{-m_{1}}G_{2}^{-m_{2}}V_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(g)
and
H_{1}H_{2}^{2}g
, where
g\in S_{(1,2),\vec{m}}(U_{1}({\mathfrak{n}}),E)
is a stabilized eigenform giving rise to
\rho
.
